ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e4458	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e4473	As far as the situation of women on the labour market is concerned, the main problem is that women are particularly active in those professions where they have very low incomes. Therefore, over the last six months, an essential measure has been to introduce a minimum wage of €1,000 in Austria for the first time, thus ensuring that there is more fairness in income especially in sectors where many women are active. As far as women are concerned, we have another problem, namely that many women have to resort to part-time jobs involuntarily, because they have no other option. Four out of five women say they do not voluntarily work part-time, but because they either do not have a suitable kindergarten for their children or because there is no other job. Another measure is that even if you work part-time, but still have to do overtime, these should be paid better. That is, overtime is overtime, whether I have a full-time service contract or a part-time service contract that 7 500 women have in Austria. And there should also be more income for these women. That means: More income-righteousness through a minimum wage, and surcharge in case of overtime also in part-time work. The third thing, which is very important to me, is the young women and the question of opportunities in the education of girls in technical, forward-looking professions. And I am very glad that the social partners have made a proposal that we have introduced apprenticeship promotion, which has started a very positive development and has been created by additional apprenticeships, now also with quality criteria, whereby we say that an apprenticeship that will give girls opportunities in a technical and future-oriented profession should also experience quality promotion. We have agreed to this in the Government Agreement, we have now put it on track. I believe that these are three very specific measures which are very important for women at work, so in the job.

In retrospect, physical fitness was suddenly much more essential than answering the questions of knowledge. That must belong to the past, because then suddenly it was so that women no longer came to the train. When I learned from the example, I said that there must also be transparency in the assessment of the tasks. - That's one thing. And it will also be published on the Internet, which skills with which evaluation should be provided. The second part is the broadcasting fee from which the new media support is to be financed. And then there are the country taxes. Since 1947, we have a financial constitution law which gives the countries autonomy in the tax system. In fact, this means that if the claim is made that the countries should not have a share, in accordance with the Financial Constitution Act, which has been valid in Austria since 1947, this requirement should be addressed to the countries and not to me as the competent Federal Minister.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e6328	Mrs Bundesminister, my question: 25/M “What conclusions do you draw from the procedure of the EU Commission against the Republic of Germany in connection with the financing of public broadcasting for the situation of the ORF?”
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e6351	Please, Mrs. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e6367	Mr Morak, the situation in Germany with ARD and ZDF is not directly comparable with the Austrian situation – especially because ARD and ZDF have very different legal conditions than we do, as you know, in the ORF law. The EU Commission's case against Germany, which was concluded with a compromise in spring 2007, has raised two problems in particular. First: ARD and ZDF need more transparency in financial matters. Payments and financing on aid and fees must be submitted to a scheme in order to increase transparency in financial flows. The second one that the EU Commission challenged in Germany was that there were no legal regulations for new media. – These were the two points the EU Commission has raised in Germany. Both points do not apply to the ORF as far as comparability is concerned. In Austria, unlike Germany, the European Transparency Directive has been implemented, including with regard to the financial controls and financial operations of the ORF. It was a failure that the Germans had not transposed the European Transparency Directive. Austria has transposed this Directive – with regard to the separation of public and commercial tasks. The second one, which was challenged in Germany, was the lack of clear legal regulations for new media. As far as online services are concerned, we have clear legal regulations in Austria, in contrast to Germany, as you know – so far as we are already pioneers throughout Europe in the field of new media and technologies, for example, the development of mobile TV. I have observed the development in Germany very closely and would finally come to the realization – that is also the opinion of my experts – that comparability is not given. I think that we can take concrete steps – including as far as EU legal frameworks are concerned – only when these concrete proposals or measures are made by the EU Commission to Austria.

Oillinger: “More thorough ”? – Not even read!]] I've already said in this place that's right. This would certainly also be a consideration in the future, so of course it will be done in the future. On the other hand, I must say that what we are discussing today has been discussed in many areas for months, weeks and many years, including the question of the introduction of the asylum court. We all oppose the fact that there is always a mixture of things that do not belong together. We are, of course, opposed to any form of illegal immigration. Of course, we believe that laws must be complied with, that law must remain right. And, of course, we believe that where crime arises, whether it is domestics or foreigners who have to fight with the means of the rule of law. So that it's clear once! It is often assumed in this whole asylum debate that it is a matter of accepting some secret form of immigration, immigration or anything else. Asylum or asylum applications are quite different. One has to imagine what that means when one is a refugee: that one has been torn out of the environment where one has grown up, where one has its social roots, where one has the circle of acquaintances, where one may even have had a job, because one cannot live there anymore because one is threatened with body and life because one is persecuted for religious, political or other reasons, and then one flees to another country in order to be able to live. There must be a strict distinction between those who suffer from this fate, who therefore also have the right and those whom we have the duty to take seriously an asylum application and to treat it in the sense of the rule of law and human rights, and that perhaps also in a time frame that is human. And then, of course, there are some who are looking at this and who leave the country without a threat to life and body, because they simply don't want to live there anymore and want to emigrate into the welfare zones. These are also available. These, of course, try to use and use all the possibilities of the rule of law. That's right, too. But if you do not want this immigration, you have to take part in a debate on a world economic order and a change in the world economy, as many people from Africa, for example, in order not to die of hunger, are beginning to migrate to the prosperity zones of Europe. But then there are other rules, that the world economy, that the World Bank, that all these institutions are taking steps – including the powerful, large industrialized countries – so that there is an order, a trade order, a world economic order that allows many who live in these continents or in these countries to see a sense of living there. Those who do not solve these problems are responsible for the fact that there are these immigration and migration flows. I myself belong to those who say: When these hikes begin, the errors that are made globally cannot be solved at the end of the day. What we have to discuss today is the question of the asylum court. I think that's a good solution. In fact, it offers the same, if not more, legal protection with a kind of special administrative court as it has been. An asylum seeker should have the possibility of being treated fairly, but quickly and quickly, so that it does not come to this congestion in the area of asylum seekers and some are waiting for a decision five, six, seven, eight, nine years. Then, in fact, it comes to saying: Now they are already integrated, now they already live here, the administration and the rule of law were unable to give them a timely notice. And all the villages then get up and say: We want them to stay! - Then they should stay there, that's no question. However, with the establishment of the Asylum Court, we have to ensure that there is at last a procedure which is human, which is handled quickly, which is fair – in the interests of the Austrians, as well as of course, in the interests of the asylum seekers. The introduction that is then negotiated before Senates that a two-directional Senate will deal with this with oral proceedings – and if they do not agree, this will deal with a five-director Senate with oral proceedings, from face to face – I think that I am a new quality, I consider a new, worthy form of legal certainty and legal protection for those who make this request. I think that's the core. Of course, the basic decisions that will help to speed up the integration of the same cases that form the basis of decision-making are all right. We still have two bodies – that has always been announced, and this is an election promise made by the Social Democrats, which is being settled by this decision today! It is also a contribution so that this discussion – the word “Arigona” – which we experienced in the last late summer, finally finds a real solution in the case of the asylum court, that such cases no longer occur and that there is simply a decision before. It is the right of the asylum seekers to represent or represent the famous NGOs. The decisions of the Asylum Court shall be published in anonymized form without delay. There's nothing secret, it's public. I think this is the right thing to do, that it will eventually be presented in this form. It will also be ensured by massive increase in staff to ensure that the first instance decisions also have a different quality. So far it has been true that in the final effect with the withdrawals, with a certain sluggishness which also arose due to this work pressure, and because of the fact that so many errors are in place, of course, there has also been a delay that all the instances have been exploited – clearly: [[Abg. Oils: The sluggishness is in the government!]] If the rule of law offers this, entire law firms have, of course, taken advantage of it, no question! But now order is to come in here: Order, legal certainty, humanity and, of course, acceleration of procedures in the interests of those who make this request and who act as asylum seekers. Another thing: There will, of course, also be an evaluation afterwards. All those who still have a certain concern today need to know that when this asylum court begins to work afterwards, if this new form – in my opinion more humanely, with more legal certainty – is active within the framework of this asylum court, there will also be an evaluation whether all that we demand here is actually fulfilled and fulfilled. I do not wish to comment on any other dispute that has taken place between the courts and many of the parties concerned. It is important that we find a solution here that gives an answer. Secondly, we are not just talking about the asylum court today: Today it is decided that the social partners should be included in the Constitution. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: “Bravo”!]] I would just like to say a few words about this: For over 50 years, there has been this social partnership. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: But not in the Constitution!]] For over 50 years, there has been criticism – with all the successes of the social partners –: In the Constitution, we have regulated Parliament, the government, the President of the Federal Republic of Germany, all these institutions. Why is social partnership moving outside the Constitution? - Time and again we heard that. Now there is an effort – and this is what we are going to conclude today – that the social partnership, which has done so much good for Austria, is also entering the Constitution. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Kammerwang in the Constitution!]] That's a clean procedure. I think that this is certainly a contribution to make this criticism, which has been repeatedly expressed, mute. It is also democratic: It will be discussed here, it will be decided here. It is not a new institution that decides laws – in order to anticipate errors – but the social partners have always had a great deal of influence and power in Austria, and I think that they too need some regulation, just as we are all regulated and just as democracy in Austria is regulated in the final effect. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Constitutional disbelief! Inevitability of the people...!]] There is no one to be put under compulsion, no one should be put under any pressure, but it is a real constitution that finds entry into the formal constitution. [[Abg. Ing. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e7338	High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The Federal Government and the government-supporting parties have decided to portion the extensive constitutional reform that is planned. A part of it has already been decided: The extension of the legislative period, the introduction of the letter election, election with 16 are already law. The second part comes now, where, at least, a very extensive constitutional adjustment is taking place, with a removal of about 1 000 constitutional provisions. I'm not going into it in detail now. This is a result of the Convention and is essentially undisputed between the political parties. There are also some important new elements: The Procuratorate is enshrined in the Constitution. The direction remains unaffected. The public prosecutor's office, which has been able to control the judicial administration so far, is given the opportunity to also make requests for deadlines where this is objectively necessary, but does not intervene in the case law, because, of course, the judicial system in its own power will decide in its own right, in its own decision. The self-administrative bodies and the social partners are included in the Constitution. A great excitement! Interestingly, the social partners are relatively silent on this issue. Whether this would have been needed in the Constitution is a matter of taste. I believe that the role of the social partners was not smaller and smaller in all the decades when there was no constitutional anchorage. But it is not bad either, because some lawyers have repeatedly complained. That is why it is now anchored. By the way, control of the social partners is also established within the framework of ministerial responsibility and also certain principles, such as the democratic composition of self-government bodies. I do not think it is bad that these principles are enshrined. There is a guarantee in the stock, not just for three, but for all self-government bodies, which is certainly an improvement. Overall, I think, little will change in the practical substance. The excitement – “back to the state of the stand” and so on – is, I mean, inappropriate. What has bothered me personally was the following: A day after we have decided by a large majority in the Constitutional Committee, your managing director has found it worth criticizing a social partners, namely the farmers' representatives, as a state-owned relic. Ladies and gentlemen, either – or! [[Abg. Neugebauer: Awesome!]] I can't! We recognise the role of the social partners, but this applies to all, including farmers, also to workers, including the economy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Of course, there is also an interesting point inside, which is now only to be criticized: Article 50 is redesigned. We therefore have the opportunity to decide not only in a two-stage procedure, but also in a one-stage procedure, but, of course, with a qualified majority. The interesting thing is – I say this in the direction of the BZÖ Members too –: This point has, of course, been reviewed. This is a subject that was discussed at the Convention. It has been sent for review for two months in July, eight weeks. There were 101 opinions. No opinion was negative. Some have even expressly given a positive opinion. It is also useful, honestly, that such ratification can be carried out in a procedure. Don't worry: Anyone who wants to vote in the public can go the way to the past, making a constitutional law, as was the case with Austria's accession. So I think that this point too is absolutely all right. The most controversial point in the public was, of course, the question of how the asylum court will continue. I think that the total and now also the reform of the azylical case law is very useful. Austria was always an open and tolerant country. We have received more than two million refugees since 1945. Most refugees, however, have gone further to other countries or back to their original homeland. This has, of course, changed now. There are many who apply for asylum without being refugees. As Josef Cap rightly said: Austria, of course, is a small wealth island in the heart of Europe, and many come for economic reasons – not incomprehensible! – to us, but they are not refugees. One thing you have to say: In 2006, for example, last year we had only one third fewer asylum applications than that of the population ten times larger Germany or twenty times larger Canada as far as the land area is concerned. We have an overhang of 34 000 procedures, of which 6 000 in the first, 28 000 in the second instance. We already increased massively last year: more than 50 employees in the first instance, 82 employees at the UBAS. It is simply not true that there is now less legal control or that the rule of law is abolished. What we all want is, of course, an acceleration of asylum procedures, a reduction in the duration of the procedure, the faster certainty also for asylum seekers on the outcome of the proceedings. And in the end: Not that bad was judged. More than 70 percent of the decisions of the asylum Senate have been confirmed by the Supreme Courts. We want this development to continue in any case. Ladies and gentlemen, therefore full support from the People's Party for this law. We hope that it will also be implemented with prudence and caution and responsibly. Ladies and gentlemen! I say quite openly: I think it is necessary that, in the interests of subsidiarity, which we always hold high, this principle be questioned very sustainably and not here opens up a development, where in a few years we may possibly have the same problems that we see today in the wake of the Balkan wars. I say quite openly to you – and this has nothing to do with party politics: I believe that we must take our work more seriously than before. It is a fundamental question whether freely elected representatives of the people, for example, agree on decision-making competence, on behalf of the people, on an international treaty, which is important but not particularly revolutionary, such as these Lisbon Treaties. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e8023	Madam President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! It is a comprehensive constitutional package, which is essentially the result of the Convention's discussions, because the Convention has completed its work and there is therefore an expert group trying to draw up proposals from this Convention outcome, which are then sent to parliamentary treatment. It is clear that a review procedure was carried out before this assignment, but it has shown that a fundamental reform of the Federal Administrative Court would take at least two years to complete. The Federal Government has therefore decided to prefer the area of the Asylum Court to deal with exactly the situation before us: that there is a setback of around 34 000 open asylum proceedings, of which only 4,000 are in the Administrative Court. These procedures take several years, and the resulting situation is unbearable. Therefore, we did not want to wait until 2010, but immediately addressed this question. The set-aside of open asylum procedures will be reduced by 2010. Asylum procedures shall not last longer than 18 months from the date of application to the final conclusion, if possible. It would also have been possible to operate with a staff increase, but after more detailed analysis we have found that this alone will not be enough. [[President Dr Spindelegger shall take over the chairmanship.]] What is it about an asylum procedure? – In this area, evidence is much more important than in all other procedures, because it is ultimately about assessing the credibility of the claim made by the applicant. It is therefore a matter of ensuring a procedure which, on the one hand, upholds the rule of law and, on the other, also leads to the completion of it in the corresponding periods of time. The asylum procedures – and it is nothing special to note – are characterised by very high appeal rates. It must be known that the appeal rates currently amount to 90 percent. And 50 percent of the asylum seekers who have a negative decision of the UBAS then appeal to the Administrative Court. So far, the Administrative Court can only go so far as to bring about a UBAS replacement decision again by the repeal. In other words, it can be done several times between UBAS and the administrative court without a final decision. What are the reasons, in fact, most of the reasons for these withdrawals of UBAS decisions by the Administrative Court? – The reasons for the withdrawal are, in particular, formal errors in the procedure, and this does not mean that all withdrawals by the Administrative Court will lead to the fact that an asylum seekers will actually receive asylum. I can also get you to know the numbers. In the years 2006 and 2007, this is as follows: In 2006, 1 227 decisions of the Administrative Court were brought against UBAS decisions. 77.1% have been confirmed and 22.9% have been withdrawn. This means that there were 281 notification fixes in 2006. This is 2.4 percent of all decisions made by the UBAS. By the way, it is not much different in 2007. The figures are available. Now a word on the decisions in which asylum was granted on the basis of the findings of the Administrative Court of Justice. This was exactly 41 cases between 2004 and 2007, 41 out of 4 740 cases decided. Of these 41 cases, a deportation protection has already been declared in 23 cases. That is, the people have not been postponed. In one case, the asylum allowance had been abolished due to an official complaint and then asylum was granted again. And in 14 cases, the assessment of the facts, such as the situation in Iran or Kosovo, was assessed differently by the Administrative Court of Kosovo, as was the case with the UBAS. [[Intrusion of the Abg. May. Brigid Weinzinger.]] It is therefore incorrect, in my opinion, to point out that the new construction deliberately reduces the recognition rate if you know how many cases it was in reality. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen, the novel that is available here, with the creation of the asylum court, is trying to address concerns in a wide range of areas. The asylum court will, in principle, decide in chambers, in chambers of two judges. This is a major improvement to the current system. And the issue of the collection of evidence and the assessment of the facts, which is so essential in asylum law, is therefore no longer carried out by an individual judge in the future, but by a Senate. Some of the two judges do not turn this case over to one chamber, which consists of five persons, and they then have to carry out the verification of the evidence and the case-law. That is, ladies and gentlemen, there is a real improvement in the legal quality of the procedures. In order to better solve the question of the same cases, we have introduced the Institute of Principle Decision. This rule of principle must be adopted by the Asylum Court whenever it wishes to take different decisions from the previous case law of the Administrative Court. I think that this is a major simplification of procedures. And a principle decision must be decided and requested by the Asylum Court in a five-person Senate. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Thus, the Administrative Court remains the highest court in matters of asylum law. This is crucial. [[Abg. Dr. Van der Bellen: I guess that's a joke! But now it's enough slowly!]] The Federal Minister for the Interior has the right to apply for such a decision in principle at the Asylum Court, and it is important that this application will have no effect on the individual case. It is therefore completely incorrect to say that there will be an imbalance in the procedure. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Of course, as in the past, the court of the Court of First Instance remains fully maintained. I am therefore convinced that, through the inclusion of the facts, by the evidence in chambers and the competence of the Administrative Court for fundamental legal issues, we have not only kept the level of protection of the law in asylum matters but also increased in addition. Now to the people and personalities who work there. The judges of the Asylum Court are judges, equipped with all the judicial guarantees provided by our Federal Constitution: They are independent, indispensable and impermissible. [[Abg. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig-Piesczek: Of course!]] It is true that we do not only provide judges as judges in the asylum court. However, it is important for the Federal Government to make the following point, and you should remember this, Madam President Glawischnig, namely that we believe that administrative officials appointed as judges also have the same qualifications as their colleagues working in the field of justice. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig-Piesczek: But your minister is looking for her!]] I would also like to say this: In fact, no one has yet come up with the idea, and I would not advise the President of the Administrative Court to pass the qualification just because he worked in the administration before, ladies and gentlemen, very much. The people appointed by the Republic as judges, no matter where, bring the qualification so that they can actually comply with their work. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig-Piesczek: That's not the point! Independence is the question!]] My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! All members of the Asylum Court shall be appointed by the Federal President on a proposal from the Federal Government. The members of the UBAS are required to take over to the Asylum Court. Dr. Glavischnig-Piesczek: Don't you notice how bizarre this is?]] Yes, Madam President, the rule of law is: Decisions will be taken, and people who are affected can take orders from the courts. – I think this is a sensible process. Furthermore, we will significantly improve documentation on the countries of origin in the Asylum Court and we will also plan agreements with other Member States of the European Union in this regard. In addition, staffing will also be significantly increased throughout the asylum area. The asylum court is raised by 24 judges and is thus the largest court of the Republic of Austria. The Administrative Court receives an additional Senate. A total of 160 persons are included in the asylum area. And if there is an additional need for scientific staff at the Constitutional Court, we will also face this challenge. So we have not only carried out the increase in staff required by many, but also changed the structures accordingly. And I am convinced that with this new construction of the asylum court, a problem that has been unresolved for many years, which has led to many conflicts and to many conflicts in Austrian society, will be eroded and solved.

Article 6 of the Convention on Human Rights states that human rights are violated if procedures take too long, as is the case in Austria. That means: On the one hand, we have a human right that is being violated, and on the other hand, we are trying to change the international standard by accelerating procedures, the national legal system. So: Do not attack human rights, but only change national law! We have to weigh between two things: between human rights on the one hand, which are being violated by too long a process, on the other hand, the change in national law. In my opinion, it is easier to bring national law into line where international standards and also our own legal needs are preserved than we are still being condemned by the European Court of Justice for the duration of the proceedings. – That is, I think, the first consideration to be met. Then you have to say: If I choose to shorten the procedure, I must comply with the rule of law. That is why I have to make an improvement in the two existing bodies. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Oils.]] That means: There is an improvement in the fact that I have the party procedure in the first instance and in the second instance, and if I have a legal issue of great importance, I have a party procedure before an enhanced Senate of five people. In my opinion, this is a much greater improvement than legal protection through a file procedure, as it cannot make direct evidence, while in the second instance evidence can be taken directly before the reinforced Senate. That means: On the one hand, we have a change in the division with an improved legal protection system and improved direct access to the party's position and, on the other hand, a violation of human rights. The Administrative Court, which in the future can no longer be invoked in individual cases, has not yet taken material decisions, but has only decided on a cassatory basis, with formal errors and procedural defects being the main reason for the annulment of judgments by the Administrative Court. I am sure that the four-eye principle and the possibility of discussing the facts in a negotiation are improvements to the current system. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In principle, or in the case of legal questions relevant to many proceedings, a five-person board of the Asylum Court of Justice will in future take a so-called 'principal decision' to be submitted to the Administrative Court, which will then have to be held within six months. If it does not decide within the time limit, the decision in principle shall be deemed to be confirmed. The Administrative Court thus receives a material decision-making competence which it has not had until now. The possibility of calling the Constitutional Court for a breach of a constitutionally guaranteed right remains. Another objective is to reduce the procedural downturn by the end of 2010. Since 2004, the recovery in asylum proceedings has been reduced: In 2005, for example, we had 42 000 open procedures, now it is 33 000 procedures. All these measures will also reduce procedural costs and the costs of basic supply. It's right: It has become more difficult for economic migrants to secure a stay through asylum procedures, which they are not legally entitled to. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e11199	The next speaker was Mrs Hlavac. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e11220	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to summarize our position on the asylum court once again: On the occasion of the decision on the foreign rights package, the SPÖ requested the establishment of an asylum court to speed up the proceedings and at the same time ensure that they: to be carried out in a fair and correct manner. That was one of the main reasons why we finally agreed to the Foreign Rights Package by a large majority. The last government has not implemented this project – there has been a motion for a resolution – but now the draft law in this area is in front of us, and I am convinced that the second instance of the asylum court will bring with it a substantial improvement. Instead of a person, two will conduct the procedure, and if they do not agree, a five-senate will meet. The applicant will thus have the right to an oral hearing before two or, if these are not agreed, even five judges. I think this is a very essential thing. This oral procedure is very central and crucial because it is precisely a matter of reviewing the credibility of the asylum seekers, and there is certainly a multi-eye principle better than what we have had so far. The decisions taken in principle, which have been very much debated in the preliminary discussion on the emergence of this law, also protect the asylum seekers. In the case of important open or disputed proceedings, the Füdersenat shall meet and take a decision of principle which shall be submitted without exception to the Administrative Court, which may then confirm or amend it. So not only the Minister of the Interior can demand it. The UNHCR could have been given the right to obtain a decision in principle, but it unfortunately rejected this right that we wanted to grant it. Of course, the lower authorities are bound by these decisions of the Administrative Court. This brings me to something quite decisive and important, namely the first instance. More than 40 percent of the decisions are lifted, so it is vital to improve the first instance. This will happen now: first, through a massive increase in human resources – there will be more lawyers – and second, by the state documentation, which is made available on a daily basis. As a result, decision-makers can better focus on individual asylum seekers. This means that both the first and the second bodies will be significantly improved, and the principle decision-making system will also help to speed up and improve procedures. This is in the interests of asylum seekers and in the interests of the rule of law at all. I have thought about all of this very well and have come to the conclusion that I too personally can accept this law.

The citizens' card, ladies and gentlemen, is not just a card but a concept behind it. And I think that this concept has ensured that, on the one hand, we will be able to implement the Services Directive in Austria at this level, so to speak, with regard to the identification of persons. On the other hand, we are once again concerned with making e-Government management as close as possible to citizens, making it as fast as possible and making it as possible as possible. I would like to thank all the ladies and gentlemen of the House who give their support to this novel package, because they also contribute to the possibility that we will have the opportunity to approach citizens with the administration, and that this will also be a better location factor for businesses. In conclusion, however, I would also like to say the following: We have very, very many dedicated people in the administration, who do not always drop the door into the castle with office closures, so to speak. It is essential for me to stress here again that the Austrian administration is not only so good because e-government works well, but because the people in the administration stand behind it and carry out these things. Otherwise, we could have the best concepts, but if the people – including officials, even the contract staff in the administration – did not participate, then we could not implement all of this. Therefore, from this official post my thanks to the employees in the public service!

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e12563	Madam President. Mrs. State Secretary! High house! The eGovernment project is an important prerequisite for the implementation of red tape reduction, shorter ways for applicants and, above all, cost-saving administrative simplification. Therefore, the objectives of these now existing eGovernment novels are, on the one hand, technical adaptation to experience since the introduction of the citizen card, on the other hand, more practical provisions on the use of the citizen card in order to force the administration to deal with it. It is true that there are still problems when it comes to use, because the use is obviously too complicated. Although we are technically leading, I would point out that it will still be necessary to see the E-Government system throughout and to ensure that there is also a change in corporate culture in the administration. An example: Unfortunately, the present draft is actually continuing its system complexity. There is a breakdown in around 40 administrative areas, and this means that the applicant must always re-establish the documents from other administrative areas. Therefore, it should be considered where information in-house is available electronically that, with the applicant's consent, the editing body could take care of the acquisition of the necessary documents, so to speak, instead of a one-stop shop, a no-stop shop. One last suggestion: By 2006 the fees for electronic files were lower, now they are the same fees again. In the context of a next amendment, consider whether this administrative simplification should also be used to reduce fees.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e14753	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! The cases of child abuse that have been reported in recent weeks have actually shaken us all. I do not believe that the fight against domestic violence and, in particular, violence against children has taken decisive action since these cases became known. Violence against children must be combated by a whole bunch of measures. Member of the European Parliament! They have also listed various social causes with which one tries to explain this phenomenon inadequately. But it must also, of course, be measures of criminal law, of civil law, of improving procedural laws, of course, but also of improving the training of the professions involved and of improving communication between the bodies employed. I immediately took action after I took office as Minister of Justice to improve measures against domestic violence and against violence against children. Although in Austria we have the Anti-Violence Act – which was a model for Europe at the time – we have to see, after ten years of existence, that the measures are still worthy of improvement. Today's urgent request gives me the opportunity to provide the House with some kind of interim report on this work and to propose further legislative measures at the same time. In cooperation with the Ministry of Family Affairs, in part also with the Ministry of the Interior, we have currently used various working groups dealing with aspects of family law, but also with the question of violence in the family. There are a large number of proposals, as they have been made by experts, and I will present some of these proposals here as well. On the subject of youth welfare, I would like to point out that this falls within the competence of my colleague Kdolsky. She told me for this debate that January will start work on a major reform of youth welfare law and that it will focus in particular on the fact that the youth welfare authorities in the countries actually proceed according to uniform standards. When we speak of violence to children, of course, the use of criminal law is also essential. In the past, a case-by-case assessment was made insofar as it was thought that the initiation of criminal proceedings against the perpetrators, who often come from the family circle, could make the situation even more difficult for the victim. This seems to me to be a wrong approach in the light of modern criminal justice. The wrong approach is that the criminal justice was seen as a threat to the victim, as is suggested by the now very flawed advertising obligations. Rightly, a determined prosecution does not preclude a gentle dealing with the child as a victim. In recent years, we have greatly expanded the protection of victims in our criminal law system. We also have some templates today where this issue plays a role. It is essential that children, when they become victims of crime, have a right to full protection by the state, to an appropriate response of the state to the violence suffered and thus to the use of criminal law. For the child as a victim, such a response by the state is a very important support in getting to grips with the experience of violence in the long term. I can now present you with some measures. On criminal law, it seems particularly important to me to make some improvements. In particular, it is necessary to consider a uniform and stricter advertising obligation for all professions and institutions dealing with children, such as youth offices, schools, kindergartens, health care and sports associations. According to the current legal situation, there is a strict reporting obligation only for the security authorities. Most other regularly confronted authorities and groups of occupations, if at all, are subject to limited reporting requirements. The general rules of the Criminal Code in § 84 StPO currently provide that an obligation to notify authorities exists if they are aware of the suspicion of a crime to be prosecuted by their legal activity. This notification obligation shall be addressed to authorities and public services represented by their directors and to which notification shall be returned to the Authority. The problem is that the obligation to display is limited to the legal scope, thus to the area of jurisdiction and the administration of sovereignty. In the context of the private administration, there is therefore no obligation to notify in accordance with the criminal procedure. As young people do not most of the tasks, they do not fulfil a general obligation to display them. [[Abg. Strache: But you have to sit there, Mrs. Justice Minister!]] They do not need to refund an advertisement even if it would affect an official activity which involved the personal trust relationship, for example, of a social worker with his client. However, there is also here – and this is already evidence of the complexity of the rule – the exception that the obligation to display is general when it is necessary to prevent further violence. Many other legal provisions provide for limited advertising obligations which are not uniform. It seems to me that these obligations should be harmonised and made more stringent. This is not only a matter for me, but also a lot of other departments and I will be happy to contact you here. It is also important to me that we show flexibility in criminal proceedings. In the first phase of state intervention and criminal law, protection of the child from further violence must be the primary objective. It will therefore be too difficult to create an opportunity to comply with criminal proceedings as far as is necessary in order to protect the victim from overload. In the course of the criminal proceedings, only those measures to protect the victim from further abuse would then be introduced. The prosecutor could therefore in future have the opportunity to carry out, under judicial control, only urgently necessary evidence – for example, in a specific case, it was so to hear an au pair girl as a witness before leaving the country again – or to take all the measures necessary to control the suspect and to secure the victim. It should also be possible if the suspect is capable of starting immediately through social therapeutic instructions, anti-violent trainings et cetera. Despite the start of the criminal proceedings, all the conditions must be ensured for a gentle handling of the victim. It is precisely by initiating criminal proceedings and the associated possibilities – for example, with a arrest of the suspect; that can be the child's father – to quickly secure the victim and protect him from further abuses. In material criminal law, we are planning to introduce a new state of action for longer-term violent relations, I have already been allowed to report this in this House. It will also be a matter of providing for certain forms of violence, such as those of children or other defenseless persons, qualifying threats of punishment. Also necessary would be the extension of the criminal provision of the quaeling or neglecting of children and defenseless persons of § 92 StGB, so that the abuse of children and of the homeless is clearly and clearly also punished there. I should also like to refer to the resolution E 13 of the National Council of 22. Reference: Council Regulation (EC) No 2168/98 of 29 March 2007 on measures to improve the system of sanctions, in particular in the field of sexual offences. Because of this resolution, we are currently evaluating in the Ministry of Justice the penalties actually imposed and the development of the frequency of the conditional redundancies of sexual offenders. – You know that the last change has taken place in this area in 2004. That is to say, the number of cases is probably not so high that one can really see a general trend here, but we are working on it and will report to Parliament as well. In accordance with the government programme, we are also working on recruitment statistics and on the relapse statistics, which I unfortunately only found very small. ==References====External links== Resolution E 19 on a professional ban on sexual criminals was adopted in May 2007. On the basis of this resolution, the Ministry of Justice has carried out a very extensive examination. We have looked at the legal situation in this area in other European countries and have already drawn up concrete proposals that we will soon be presenting. I should also like to point out that, in the course of one of the proposals that we are still being discussed here today in the context of the reform of the sanctions law, there is also an upgrading of the assessment body for sexual offenders. This assessment body is currently conducting therapy with sexual offenders as part of the criminal execution. Where these therapies are taking place today, it is true that the rate of relapse is drastically reduced. – We will generally expand this assessment body and use it much more for assessments in connection with conditional redundancies. Now for victim protection: When children become victims, effective victim protection is particularly important. Here, too, I should like to point out that with the criminal code, the one with 1. It will come into force in January, and its accompanying legislation will be in place today to decide on the protection of victims from 1. ==References====External links== I should like to stress some of the rules: The gentle agreement of the victim in criminal proceedings will be extended, a central coordination body for victims of criminal offences that can be reached by telephone around the clock is available, there is free legal and psychosocial process support, there is the possibility of private parties connecting in criminal proceedings and new information and legal means for victims. I would like to point out at this point that a good system of victim protection is also an important preventive instrument: Especially children who become victims of violence will often become perpetrators themselves later if they have not experienced good care as a victim. I also see important measures in the organisational field. We will therefore set up special responsibilities and specialists, particularly in the major prosecutors, who take on the subject of violence in the family. We have also provided, in a proposal which is in place today, that a much higher consideration should also be given to the training and training of judges and prosecutors of the whole subject complex of domestic violence and dealing with the victims than has been the case so far. There is also a need for action in civil law: We will ensure in civil law that the victim also has the right to legal and psychosocial process support. A note from the practice, which is very important and which we want to comply with, is that the address of the victim's residence is kept secret and is not given to the perpetrator. And the right to gentle consent of the victim is also granted in the civil process. We want to allow for a longer period of validity in respect of the interim orders of the Family Court created in the Violence Law. We are working on an action plan to improve family jurisdiction – I am aware of the fact that there are repeated complaints here. The judges also take this issue very active on their own initiative and have made themselves the theme of family law and family jurisdiction for their judicial week 2008. Now on the right to visit and the decisions on care: You know that, of course, exactly in this area, very often the recovery of experts is necessary, which takes some time in the process. However, the law provides that it may lead to the temporary transfer of care if this is necessary for the child's well-being. On behalf of the Ministry of Justice, you have already mentioned the study, which has been carried out on behalf of the Ministry of Justice. The study has concluded that in some 54 percent of cases parents opt for common care after divorce. – In fact, the experience is generally very positive, so we do not plan any legal changes in this area. [[Abg. Mr Strache: Mrs Minister for Justice! It should also be extended to the other percent!]] - I'll be right there. By the way, the evaluation has also resulted – which seems to me to be a very interesting aspect – that the fathers often care more about the children after the divorce than they do when they are genuinely married. So we should also take this into account. The common care is of course - and this is the main theme of today's day - not in question when violence is in play. In this respect, also in Germany, the common care is not the absolute rule, but it is, as it were, subject to restrictions. Our regulations are more or less similar to that in their effects. [[Abg. Mr Strache: Mrs Minister for Justice! It is the rule case only if it can be proved...! When it comes to violence, don't!]] The fight against violence against children is indeed a personal concern and an important part of government work. I should like to remind you that we in the government have not yet had joint activities in which three or four ministerial colleagues were involved. For my area of responsibility, I have outlined the measures already implemented or planned to combat violence on children and will also seek to discuss this issue with my group members and support further joint initiatives. Thank you.

It is often physical violence, which may not be visible as such, because it leaves no traces, because ear figs are simply not so debilitating, or because they are small injuries, which are not recognized by the child environment as abuses, because they are scrapping or perhaps torn-off hair. It is also difficult that such signs may be clues, but no evidence. We are facing the problem that violence against children can be a psychological, a physical force, that sexual assaults may occur, and that our common effort needs to create framework conditions that help children to their right. We also have the phenomena that children love their parents, even if they are abusive parents, even if they are parents who appear to their children in a humiliating way. It is the children who often make themselves responsible for it when there are problems between the parents or in the parent-child relationship. We have unlimited reporting obligations for safety authorities, but we also have to report to the Youth Office for medical-technical or medical professions, for physiotherapists, psychotherapists and so on, but they are also subject to a great duty of confidentiality. And of course in that is also a certain discrepancy. Since Luca's case, the series of child abuses has not been torn off, at least they are published in the media accordingly. And above all, we are emotionally extremely affected because it is simply cases where one can also feel the difficult situation of these young children because of inner distress. Of course, there are also questions as to how it really looks with our system, which is responsible here, how it works, if there are shortcomings where you have to start. How do our social workers manage in daily life? Whether there are trust relationships with the families where fears exist to destroy them if you might make ads? Or is the sense of when borders are exceeded? Or how do you set these limits in order to really make timely ads? Or is there a lack of courage to really bring in ads? The main task of the Youth Office must always be to protect the children, who must be in the foreground, and above all to protect against further violence when violence has already come. We currently have authorities, but also outpatients and doctors, who are very sensitive and who are also doing any injury. I have experienced such a case in my own acquaintance, where a child who has been turned around on a bicycle stand has slipped and has come to the hospital due to a nasal fracture, with the mother of three different persons being asked about the accident. That is why it is also a very sensitive issue, where you also need to question a general advertising obligation. This mother was wise and, of course, knew very well that it is about preventing violence against children. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e17779	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! For several weeks now, we have had a debate on transparency in political incomes, and therefore also on transparency in party funding and in party donations. Austria, which has to be foreseen, makes relatively large investments in the political system. Hubert Sickinger has tried to investigate this in 2005 or has investigated it and has found in a study that at that time about €150 million per year of federal government and countries – as the communities are not yet involved – go to the political system, that is to say to the parties at the federal and national level. This is a lot, compared to other countries. We are in the absolute lead here as regards the financing of the political parties. As far as Members' salaries are concerned, we are also in the forefront. Although we cannot measure ourselves with some managers in the industry, we still have to say that we deserve a lot of what men and even women deserve on average. We move there about in the order of five to seven times. This can be referred to as a proper remuneration for the activities of Members. We think that's right. We think that, rightly, a lot of money goes into the Austrian political system, because it has great advantages. The objective of this funding is the independence of political decisions. The aim is to prevent corruption and political decisions that have been bought. This objective applies both to parties and to Members, and one has to invest a little bit, that this probability of corruption, for the purchase of It is a matter of making a little less political decisions. That works in general, you can say, in Austria still relatively good. That is to say, the dependence on donations should be pushed back. However, if one approves this, one must also see that the public has a right to get clarity about the use of the funds, that the public has a right to see if the target that is being stated is really achieved. The key requirements here are transparency and the public; Transparency of what money flows to where, and the public in the sense that the public is also informed about it. It is precisely this transparency that is not guaranteed in the current legislation in Austria. The Party Law is completely inadequate and far away from any international standard, especially as regards the rules relating to donations. Short of the state of things: What obligation do parties currently have to disclose, i.e. really to publish what they do with tax money? – The parties must give an account once a year, which is published in the “Wiener Zeitung”. However, there is no detailed statement there, there is a gross revenue and expenditure account. If some politicians – mainly the major parties – claim that the Court of Auditors would have a means of control, for example as regards donations, it is simply and simply a misinformation – so as not to risk a call for order here. Provision is that donations over €7 260 must be declared. However, this is a rubber paragraph, as you say so beautifully Austrian. On the one hand, there are a lot of exceptions, on the other hand, there is no provision to prevent a fund split. That is, you have every opportunity to take in donations, which, for example, amount to € 7 000 ten times a year. As long as you stay under this €7,200, you don't even need to announce it to the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors must not publish it anyway, but not even the notification to the Court of Auditors is necessary. That is to say, this donation division is currently possible and will certainly be managed as far as the political scientist Hubert Sickinger, who has been working on it for many years, can say that. In addition, there are a lot of exceptions, and they are really interesting. Exceptions – that is, there must not even be a publication of these donations, even if they go over €7 260 – are there when it comes to public law bodies or voluntary membership-based professional and economic associations, institutions, foundations and funds. – In short and concisely, as two examples, Industrial Union and Austrian Trade Union Confederation. There is no need to mention the donations in this account report at all. Exception two: Donations to the parties' advance or partial organisations. In this context, Lorenz Fritz made an interesting comment in the Eurofighter Investigation Committee as President of the Industrial Association. It has admitted in this investigation committee that donations from third parties to the ÖVP or ÖAAB have been made through the Industrial Association. That is to say, he has de facto admitted money laundering on the industrial union. This is precisely our criticism point and also the criticism point of the Court of Auditors – for example, the Council of Europe – that in Austria it is possible to wash donations through preparatory organisations, through public law agencies, and that there is no obligation in Austrian law to make these donations transparent. The Council of Europe has already published proposals in 2003 for disclosure of party donations, for limiting the costs of the election campaign and for sanctions, if these provisions to come into force are not complied with. But apparently the big parties do not care much. We Greens have been voting in favour of these transparency criteria for years. For years now, we have been fighting for a change of law here – not to any washed-up goodwill actions, but to clear legal regulations, which are also comprehensible to the public. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Finally, we did this with two requests. Already in October 2006, before the National Council elections, we made two applications, one for the election cost limitation and one for the glass party funds. Both applications are now well isolated, for a year as usual, but have now found the way into the Constitutional Committee, where they – surprise! – have been postponed! These proposals refer essentially to a detailed presentation of the party revenue in the accounting report, no donations of more than €500, which are anonymous – i.e. a ban on donations of over €500, if not shown where they come from –, prevention of fundraising through forwarding through various bodies of anonymous donations and no donation split. The problem is that the ÖVP sees itself on this issue as a representative of transparency, concealment and blockade and condemns every demand for control here as a Majesty's policy, while the SPÖ apparently bows down to this Majesty with joy and is not inclined to really enforce what it claims to be. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] This culminates in such bizarre situations as that Klubobmann Cap says in an interview that the SPÖ has a request in Parliament and that it will see that something is going on right now. . It also concerns the Additional Protocol to the Convention on the Protection of Human Beings in the Automatic Processing of Personal Data concerning Control Units and Cross-border Data Transport. – This Convention of the Council of Europe on the Protection of Human Rights in the Automatic Processing of Personal Data is actually the most important multilateral legal basis for general data protection in Europe. An additional protocol, in 147 of the supplements to which Austria is now to join, regulates cross-border traffic and its control. The accession of Austria is intended to require the establishment of a national and independent data protection authority at the Council of Europe. For Austria itself, such regulations have long existed under the Data Protection Act. It is also regulated by the protocol of international traffic for Austria. Matter is positive and finds our consent. Thank you.

It is therefore an important signal because, on the one hand, we have to assume that international crime, terrorism, too, must be combated internationally, also by the fact that individual countries exchange their existing data, partly through their own citizens, where there is suspicion of criminal acts or organisations. Of course, citizens must also have European recognised rights, which at least make the misuse of these data difficult, if not – which would be desirable – to prevent. In this context, however, I would like to point to a problem which the Federal Government now wants to decide in Austria, in a Husch-Puff action, when amending the Security Police Act and some other materials: There should now be comprehensive mobile surveys, even without judicial arrangement. It is not at all clear which barriers will exist, which controls will exist. I always say that the border is far, and a righteous citizen does not need to be fear that there are cameras somewhere, surveillance et cetera, but there are still limits. But that security authorities can beat every Austrian mobile phone without any barriers and know exactly where the person is right now, that already sounds like “1984” by George Orwell, and that is what we want, even if there is international terrorism, even if there is international crime, here in Austria.

There is an EU Framework Decision of 25 December 1990. ==References====External links== The decision provides for criminal frameworks which are now to be implemented, mainly on the subject of strengthening the criminal frameworks. There is an increase in the threat of punishment for several acts of action. There is a decreed criminality in the cultivation of opium, poppy, coca and cannabis plants, provided that they are used for the production of addiction. The mushrooms with the active substance psilocybin are also banned. There are skill statistics in the case of committed offences to place addiction on the market. Nevertheless, the topic of “treatment instead of punishment” has been implemented very successfully in Austria. There is the provisional withdrawal of the persecution according to § 35. It's going to be extended here. That is to say, there is the possibility – and this is always the most promising thing about criminal measures – to put in place and look at a correspondingly tailored measure in each individual case: What is the best way to deal with the person concerned? With what probability can one prevent a relapse? What is the most targeted instrument here? So this has been drawn up here in the draft, so that we have put treatment in the foreground instead of punishment, in addition to the imperative needs in the framework decision. We therefore believe that the overall package will take a further step towards a reasonable, efficient judicial care. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e20049	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! High house! We all in our country expect optimum security from a state. It is therefore necessary to rethink and rationalise the principles of criminal enforcement. Many aspects are now taken into account in this new law. There is agreement on the need to force the reintegration of convicted persons into society in order to reduce the relapse rate. Several opportunities arise, such as the change in the event of a conditional dismissal. On the one hand, general-preventive grounds of refusal should be used only more limitedly, on the other hand, the requirement for redundancies of at least half the level of punishment is re-regulated. The aim is to ensure adequate supervision and best control of the aid, which is compulsory. The rewording of Sections 50 and 52 will help to improve the effectiveness of the instruments of probation aid. I think it is good that we should look at each individual case individually in case of a conditional release of detention and treat it accordingly, because the parole aid must be effective support for the prisoner and also seen as a protection for society. It is a common approach at European Union level to the fight against illegal trade. Unfortunately, the framework decision does not cover the personal consumption of addiction drugs. In the context of the Framework Decision, the criminal framework for drug trafficking is constantly increasing. The main aim is to strengthen the fight against drug trafficking and to strengthen the rules on drugs. However, I would like to focus on the principle: Strengthen the principle of “therapy instead of punishment”. § 35 of the Addictions Act should further assist addict consumers through health policy measures. In this sense, help takes precedence over punishment. By strengthening the principle of “therapy instead of punishment”, those concerned should be quick to implement the therapeutic measures. This principle is of the utmost importance. This is recognised by the fact that under § 41 in 2006, almost €5 million were spent on the expenses of the judicial system for these therapy measures. I can also describe my own experiences here, because there is such a treatment centre, operated by the Green Circle, in my immediate vicinity, and I must note that the number of people working there is increasing rapidly, but also the number of those affected. The Green Circle has begun with a number of 14 people, and within three years this number has increased to 102. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e21061	The next speaker is Mrs Stadlbauer with 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e21082	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! High house! With the various legislative drafts available to the plenary today and tomorrow, a completely new role model of the prosecutors is created. The Criminal Process Reform Act, adopted in 2004, took place on 1 January 2004. In January 2008, criminal proceedings will be reorganised. The prosecutor or the prosecutor becomes the central figure in the new preliminary proceedings. It is important to mention that the prosecutors will receive a personal increase of about 50 percent. The additional planning posts have been continuously advertised and occupied since the middle of this year. The new strong role of the public prosecutors corresponds to the fact that we first record the prosecutors in the federal constitution today. This not only strengthens the status of prosecutors, but also the overall judicial system. This constitutional security of prosecutors is supplemented by a realignment of the law. As early as January 2008, the public prosecutor's decision-making processes will be made transparent by exposing all the instructions of the Minister of Justice and the Supreme Public Prosecutor's Offices in the future. In the future, a statement of any instructions on the files of the prosecutor's office or of the court is to be taken. The instructions are therefore also subject to file inspection. And if a procedure has not become judicial, the Minister of Justice will report this to the National Council annually. This avoids any appearance of political influence on the prosecutor's office. I also think it is particularly important to adapt the legal service regulations for the prosecutors. This will be discussed in plenary tomorrow. Public prosecutors and prosecutors are thus also formally treated as judges in their importance. All in all, they come from 1st grade. January 2008 is a completely new type of prosecutor/an attorney. He or she is redefined in four dimensions: constitutional as an institution of jurisdiction, procedurally as a successor of the investigating judge in the role of the head in the investigation in the preliminary proceedings, under the law of law, by including in the judicial service law in a position very close to the judges, and by strengthening the position of the prosecutors in relation to the Minister of Justice through a strong restriction of reporting obligations and the disclosure of ministerial instructions. All in all, there is no doubt that the largest reform of the public prosecutor's institution since its creation in 1873 can be spoken of. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e22819	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! The situation of blind people has changed greatly in recent years. This is particularly true of the professional perspective. While previously blind people had only the chance to become broomers or telephone operators, almost all professional sectors are open to them today. We have also deleted in the Disability Equality Act vocational access restrictions such as the physical fitness prescribed for the judicial profession or the teacher's profession. This too should be possible now. There are blind people today who are both entrepreneurs and lawyers. At the same time, there was the provision of protection, which proved to be discrimination, that blind people should not be allowed to sign lawfully. So even lawyers have not been allowed to sign contracts, to buy a flat or to open a bank account. This has now been eliminated. There are now no more notarial acts, blind people can give up on them. However, there is still the possibility to take the advice of the Notarial Chamber and to sign contracts here in good advice. I believe that this is a very good solution, and discrimination will be eliminated here. At this point I would also like to thank the officials in the Ministry of Justice for being open and showing themselves very mediating between disabled people and the Notary Chamber. Thank you.

Point Report of the Committee on Education on the Government Template (259 e.g._Other Organiser Federal Act amending the Education Documentation Act (379 d.a.)
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23022	We're entering the 20th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker is Mr Brosz. 5 minutes of voluntary time limit. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-05-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23049	Madam President. Mrs. Minister! So today the novel to the Education Documentation Act is on the agenda. The SPÖ will probably now explain to us that the next century throw will take place. If we look back a little over the past few years, we see that there have been various debates, parliamentary questions and requests here in the House – many of us, even more from the SPÖ. The criticism of this was quite uniform, namely that the link between the social security number and educational data stored over a long period of time is unacceptable and that this should be changed. Today, we have adopted an international agreement to improve the development of data protection regulations, and some speakers have said that there is no need for change in Austria, because the Data Protection Council is already installed. However, the Data Protection Council noted in the assessment of the Education Documentation Act that the link between the educational data and the social security number is unacceptable. However, we are now presented with the novel to the Education Documentation Act, in which the link between the social security number and the educational data has been maintained. And not just that: In the past, the SPÖ – Mrs. Wurm, Mr. Maier – has repeatedly criticised the fact that religious confession is also raised and stored. We are now being presented with the novel to the Education Documentation Act, and the religious confession is of course still being raised. I would like to ask you, at least on this point, which would be relatively simple to change, which has a real opportunity to change: [[Quoted print version removed]] This would no longer raise the religious confession. Of course, there are some improvements in the law. This is not too difficult, since it was then a law under massive criticism. Among other things, access rights are restricted to those who in principle had the opportunity to access the data, i.e. insurance or pension rights or family allowances. There was a provision that thank God never came into force, that data can be accessed. It's getting out of here now. We shall also vote in the separate vote on this point. However, the core, the social security number, remains unfortunately. This is incomprehensible in that a two-part system is already being used. There are children who have a social security number and children who do not have a social security number. In this case, a replacement mark is already awarded. At present, there are about 20,000 replacement marks if the data are still up-to-date. Obviously, it works for those with a replacement mark, but not for all 1.2 million with a different label from the social security number. In other words, two parallel systems are apparently more efficient and easier to conduct than a common one in which one works with a different label. I have not understood this conclusion and this logic so far. Obviously you are pressing a little bit the bad conscience, because it is also in the law that Parliament should be presented with a different regulation within the next two years – without a social security number. What I find particularly annoying – because I look at my colleague Maier: The most intense point is, I think, the supplement to the question of what happens if the social security number is not announced by the parents. That's pretty violent. So far, we have had the following provision: If it is not announced, there is an administrative penalty threat. It should have been pursued rigorously, and only for those who do not have a social security number would the replacement mark have been valid. Now there are obviously thousands of parents who have said – like colleague Maier or colleague Worm in the last few periods – that is sensitive data, we do not announce this number. Obviously, they have usually been pushed to the eye, for there have been relatively few punishments. And what happens in the novelle now? – Now they say you have to announce the number already, but if you don’t give it up, it doesn’t matter: We do not punish you, but then the Statistics Austria may extract the social security number from the registration register and associate it with the data. So not that you delete it or say we don't use the social security number anymore: The parents are simply and simply bypassed in the current system, and Statistics Austria, which is now responsible for encryption, can collect the data from another register and link education data with it. I think this is a process which is extremely worrying by data protection law. There's not much to point out about this either. That is paragraph 7 in the law, Mr Niederweiser, which can be viewed relatively closely. The reason you provide is: This is much more harmless now, because it will be encrypted differently – that is partly true – with Statistics Austria. [[Intervocations at the ÖVP.]] - That's right, yeah, okay. However, you can return to the same number at any time with the social security number – even after 30 years, even if it has been deleted in the law – via the same encryption. That's not a secret story in the sense that's happening there: The encryption always results in the same encoding, and whether or not the social security number is erased or not is irrelevant, because it continues to exist, and everyone who encrypts again via the social security number comes to the same data. The fact is, therefore, that the merger of social security numbers with sensitive educational data remains stable. It will partly reduce the storage time, even if it is true, but in principle one has to say that after a year of this government I would have imagined more. There are seven opinions in the review procedure against the link with the social security number: It is advised by the colleague Buchinger not to associate with the social security number; the Data Protection Council advises not to link with the social security number; the main association of social security insurance – which also has something to do with social security numbers – says that one should not link to it. So there are all these contradictions. The Constitutional Service of the Federal Chancellor's Office, which we will always hear if you legitimize what the procedure is supposed to be, is against the link. All the relevant institutions that had to do with this have therefore advised to leave that and to solve it differently. But it remains in the law. That is why we cannot accept this amendment either.
